class Solution:
  def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool
    idx = defaultdict(deque)
    for i, c in enumerate(s):
      idx[int(c)].append(i)
    for c in t:
      d = int(c)
      if not idx[d]: return False
    idx[d].popleft()
    return True

def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
    pos = defaultdict(deque)
    for i,ch in enumerate(s):
        pos[int(ch)].append(i)
    for ch in reversed(t):
        d = int(ch)
        if not pos[d]: return False
        p = pos[d][0]
        pos[d].popleft()

            if pos[smaller] and pos[smaller][0] < p:

    return True
```

```
def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
    pos = collections.defaultdict(deque)
    for i, ch in enumerate(s):
        pos[int(ch)].append(i)
    for ch in t:
        d = int(ch)
        if not pos[d]: return False
        p = pos[d][0]
        for i in range(d):
            if pos[i] and pos[i][0] < p:
                return False
        pos[d].popleft()
    return True
```

```
class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        n = len(s)
        pos = [[] for _ in range(10)]
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            pos[int(ch)].append(i)
        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if not pos[d]:
                return False
            p = pos[d][0]
            for i in range(d):
                if pos[i] and pos[i][0] < p:
                    return False
            pos[d].pop(0)
        return True
```

Operation: choose any substring and sort ascending. This is equivalent to applying adjacent swaps where a smaller digit can swap left with a larger digit. Because sorting a substring can be decomposed into adjacent inversions: if adjacent pair (x,y) with x>y, sorting length-2 substring swaps to y,x. So allowed operation: swap adjacent inverted pair (larger before smaller) to (smaller before larger). Thus a smaller digit can move left by swapping with larger digits; a larger digit can move right by swapping with smaller digits. No operation can swap a smaller before larger to larger before smaller. Therefore the relative order of equal digits? Equal no swap. More importantly, for any pair of digits a<b, if a appears before b in s, then in t can b appear before a? To make b before a, need swap b left across a or a right across b. Since a<b, adjacent pair (b,a) is inverted (larger before smaller), allowed to swap to (a,b), which would make a before b, not b before a. To get b before a, need swap (a,b) (correct order) to (b,a), not allowed. So if a<b and a is before b in s, then a must remain before b in t? Thus for any pair of values x<y, if x is before y in s, then x must be before y in t. Conversely, if x is after y in s (y before x), then it's possible to swap to x before y, but not required. So a necessary and sufficient condition? For all pairs x<y, the relative order of x and y in t cannot have y before x if x was before y in s. class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        pos = collections.defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            pos[int(ch)].append(i)
        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if not pos[d]:
                return False
            p = pos[d][0]
            for i in range(d):
                if pos[i] and pos[i][0] < p:
                    return False
            pos[d].popleft()
        return True
```

Let's craft final code:
```
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        idx = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, c in enumerate(s):
            idx[int(c)].append(i)

        for c in t:
            d = int(c)
            if not idx[d]:
                return False
            p = idx[d][0]
            for smaller in range(d):
                if idx[smaller] and idx[smaller][0] < p:
                    return False
            idx[d].popleft()

        return True
```
Need check if `idx[smaller]` creates empty deque for smaller keys? `defaultdict(deque)` will create empty deque when accessed, but that's okay. But in loop `for smaller in range(d): if idx[smaller] and ...` if no smaller, creates empty deque entries. Not harmful. Could use `if idx[smaller] and idx[smaller][0] < p`. Good.

Need maybe use `pos = [deque() for _ in range(10)]` to avoid defaultdict import? But defaultdict/deque fine. Need ensure no issue with `defaultdict(deque)` and `if not idx[d]`: if key absent, creates empty deque and returns False. That's okay. But if d absent, creates empty deque; no harm. However in `for smaller`, accessing idx[smaller] creates empty deques for absent smaller digits. Fine.

Let's maybe search memory: LeetCode 1585 solution in C++:
```
bool isTransformable(string s, string t) {
    vector<deque<int>> pos(10);
    for (int i = 0; i < s.size(); ++i)
        pos[s[i]-'0'].push_back(i);
    for (char c : t) {
        int d = c - '0';
        if (pos[d].empty()) return false;
        int p = pos[d].front();
        for (int i = 0; i < d; ++i) {
            if (!pos[i].empty() && pos[i].front() < p)
                return false;
        }
        pos[d].pop_front();
    }
    return true;
}
```
Yes.

from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        idx = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, c in enumerate(s):
            idx[int(c)].append(i)

        for c in t:
            d = int(c)
            if not idx[d]:
                return False
            p = idx[d][0]
            for smaller in range(d):
                if idx[smaller] and idx[smaller][0] < p:
                    return False
            idx[d].popleft()

        return True

Need maybe consider if `idx = defaultdict(deque)` should be `idx = [deque() for _ in range(10)]` to avoid defaultdict side effects. Both fine. If using list, no import defaultdict. But original used defaultdict. We can fix with list of deques:
```
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False
        idx = [deque() for _ in range(10)]
        for i, c in enumerate(s):
            idx[int(c)].append(i)
        for c in t:
            d = int(c)
            if not idx[d]:
                return False
            p = idx[d][0]
            for smaller in range(d):
                if idx[smaller] and idx[smaller][0] < p:
                    return False
            idx[d].popleft()
        return True
```

Let's decide final code:
```
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        pos = [deque() for _ in range(10)]
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            pos[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if not pos[d]:
                return False

            p = pos[d][0]
            for smaller in range(d):
                if pos[smaller] and pos[smaller][0] < p:
                    return False

            pos[d].popleft()

        return True
```
This is clean. But original variable `idx`; either. Use `pos` or `idx`. I'll use `idx` to stay close:
```
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        idx = [deque() for _ in range(10)]
        for i, c in enumerate(s):
            idx[int(c)].append(i)

        for c in t:
            d = int(c)
            if not idx[d]:
                return False

            p = idx[d][0]
            for smaller in range(d):
                if idx[smaller] and idx[smaller][0] < p:
                    return False

            idx[d].popleft()

        return True
```

```
vector<deque<int>> pos(10);
for (int i = 0; i < s.size(); ++i)
    pos[s[i]-'0'].push_back(i);
for (char c : t) {
    int d = c-'0';
    if (pos[d].empty()) return false;
    for (int i = 0; i < d; ++i) {
        if (!pos[i].empty() && pos[i].front() < pos[d].front())
            return false;
    }
    pos[d].pop_front();
}

```

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        idx = [deque() for _ in range(10)]
        for i, c in enumerate(s):
            idx[int(c)].append(i)

        for c in t:
            d = int(c)
            if not idx[d]:
                return False

            p = idx[d][0]
            for smaller in range(d):
                if idx[smaller] and idx[smaller][0] < p:
                    return False

            idx[d].popleft()

        return True
```

Need maybe if `s` and `t` are same length but t contains digit not in s, false. Good.

Let's maybe think if using `idx[smaller]` in loop creates no side effects. List no side effects. Good.

Let's maybe think if there is a scenario where `len(s) != len(t)` but transformation possible by sorting? No, sorting preserves length. Good.

Let's maybe think if there is a scenario where `s` and `t` have same length but algorithm returns false incorrectly due to using earliest occurrence of d and not considering that a smaller digit before p could be moved right across d? Wait smaller digit x<d before p in s. To have d before x in t, need x move right across d or d move left across x. Since x<d, sorting ascending puts x before d. The pair (x,d) is already in correct order in s (x before d), and no operation can make d before x because that would require swapping a smaller-before-larger pair to larger-before-smaller, which is not allowed. So impossible. Thus fail correct.

What if x before p in s but x is not the same occurrence that would be before d in t? Equal digits indistinguishable; if any x before p remains, then there is an x value before d in s that hasn't been placed before d in t. If some other x after p is placed before d, that doesn't help because the x before p still must appear somewhere. If it appears after d, then pair violation for that occurrence. So fail.

Let's maybe think if there is a scenario where x before p in s but x target after d in t, yet x before p in s can be moved right across d? x<d, x before d, cannot. So fail.

Thus correct.

Let's maybe think if there is a scenario where x after p in s and x target after d in t, no issue. If x target before d in t, allowed because x<d and d before x in s can swap. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
is okay. Need final code only. I'll output code.</think>```python
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        idx = [deque() for _ in range(10)]
        for i, c in enumerate(s):
            idx[int(c)].append(i)

        for c in t:
            d = int(c)
            if not idx[d]:
                return False

            p = idx[d][0]
            for smaller in range(d):
                if idx[smaller] and idx[smaller][0] < p:
                    return False

            idx[d].popleft()

        return True
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        idx = [deque() for _ in range(10)]
        for i, c in enumerate(s):
            idx[int(c)].append(i)

        for c in t:
            d = int(c)
            if not idx[d]:
                return False

            p = idx[d][0]
            for smaller in range(d):
                if idx[smaller] and idx[smaller][0] < p:
                    return False

            idx[d].popleft()

        return True
